Oh, well... I wouldn't say I've lost interest completely, no. I mean, I still love my hobbies and my family dearly! But... I suppose, um, I haven't really been wanting to go to my dance class the past couple of weeks. Or go look at the little galleries like I usually do.

Well, I wouldn't say I've completely stopped eating or anything drastic like that! I certainly don't want you to worry about me starving myself. 

But, um... I suppose my eating has been just a little bit off lately. I find that just having my morning coffee and maybe a few little bites of something is enough to carry me through most of the day. And then at night... well, I feel a little silly admitting this, but I just sort of pick at some tortillas or maybe a few sweets. And I don't even really enjoy them the way I usually would, I'm just kind of nibbling without much thought. 

I make sure I'm still getting something in my stomach, though! I know how important it is to keep my strength up, especially at my age. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope you don't think I'm developing terrible habits, I just haven't had my usual appetite for proper meals.

I hope I'm explaining it okay for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no, I wouldn't say depressed. I try very hard to keep a smile on, you know? Oh, well... I wouldn't say I feel like a burden or anything quite that strong. I know my family loves me, and I certainly don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party!

Oh, goodness, no! I mean, I would never actually do anything to harm myself. I really don't want you to worry about me in that way, because I absolutely wouldn't do that. I have no plan to do anything like that whatsoever.

Well... I wouldn't say I'm moving like a turtle or anything! I definitely don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. I try very hard to stay active and keep up with everything. 

But, um, I suppose... when I'm just at home, by myself, sometimes my legs do feel just a tiny bit heavy. Like I'm moving a little slower than I usually do. But then, when I'm at work, especially in meetings with the residents or contractors, it's almost the opposite! I catch myself tapping my pen or shifting around in my chair. I get this... this keyed up feeling, like I just can't quite sit still.

And I've been asking my assistant to just double-check some of the vendor details for me. She's such a dear, she doesn't mind at all. And I did push a couple of minor board items to next month's agenda, just because... well, I guess I just haven't been feeling quite as sharp, and I don't want to make a mistake and cause a fuss for anyone. I just don't quite trust my judgment right this second with that cloudy feeling. \n\nBut I'm still getting everything done! I really hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm slacking off, Doctor. I keep thinking I've let them down by getting their hopes up. Nobody has actually complained or said I ruined anything! They're all very sweet people. Oh, well, I wouldn't use the word distress. That sounds so severe, doesn't it? I really don't want to complain, because I know people have much bigger problems. I'm still getting to work every day, and I'm keeping up with things as best I can.

I really hope I don't sound like a terrible wife for saying that his being home all day might have triggered it! I love having him around, I really do, it's just... a big adjustment, I suppose. Is it normal for something like a retirement to cause a little slump like this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble with your memory?\n\nPatient: Memory? Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I certainly haven't forgotten any important meetings or misplaced my keys or anything dreadful like that! \n\nI suppose I might be just a tiny bit more forgetful than usual, but really, who isn't at my age? Sometimes I'll walk into a room and have to think for a second about what I went in there for, but it always comes right back to me. And like I said, my assistant has been helping me keep track of the little details at work, so nothing is slipping through the cracks. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything! I'm still perfectly capable of keeping everything organized. Does that sound alright? I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I've lost interest completely, no. I mean, I still love my hobbies and my family dearly! But... I suppose, um, I haven't really been wanting to go to my dance class the past couple of weeks. I really hope you don't think I'm being lazy or neglecting my family, Doctor! I promise I plan to get back to it all soon. Is it okay to just... step back from those things for a little bit while I'm feeling this way?\n\nDoctor: Since this cloudy feeling started, has your appetite gone down?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I've completely stopped eating or anything drastic like that! I know how important it is to keep my strength up, especially at my age. Does that make sense, Doctor? Oh, um... weight? Well, I haven't actually stepped on a scale, so I couldn't say for sure about any numbers. I certainly don't want to look like I'm wasting away or anything!

I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Did this cloudy feeling start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose there was a little change recently, but I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining about my husband! He's a good man. It's just that he retired about two weeks ago, and suddenly he's home all day, every day. \n\nIt was right around then that I started feeling this... well, this sharper shift. First my energy and my confidence were just so different, and then this low, cloudy feeling settled in. It's been about two weeks now. I hope it doesn't sound awful of me to say that his being home might have rattled me a bit? I just want to be honest with you, Doctor. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling sad?\n\nPatient: Sad? Oh, no, I wouldn't use that word at all. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying or anything like that. I'm not a weeping willow, I promise! \n\nLike I said, it's just this... cloudy feeling. A bit of a slump. But not sad. I try to keep my chin up and stay positive, you know? I wouldn't want to bring anyone else down by being sad. I just feel a little less... bright, I suppose. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has this cloudy feeling been making it harder for you to do your job the way you normally do?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say it's making it *hard* exactly. I'm still going in to work every day, of course! I manage a few community associations, and I would never want to let my residents or the boards down. I'm very dedicated to my job. \n\nBut, um, I suppose I have been taking things just a little bit slower lately. Nothing terrible! Just... taking my time with decisions, you know? And I've been asking my assistant to just double-check some of the vendor details for me. She's such a dear, she doesn't mind at all. And I did push a couple of minor board items to next month's agenda, just because... well, I guess I just haven't been feeling quite as sharp, and I don't want to make a mistake and cause a fuss for anyone. I just don't quite trust my judgment right this second with that cloudy feeling. \n\nBut I'm still getting everything done! I really hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm slacking off, Doctor. I'm still working very hard, I promise. Is it okay that I'm leaning on my assistant a little bit right now?\n\nDoctor: Did this cloudy feeling begin after your husband retired and started being home during the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly. I think I mentioned it earlier, but yes, it's been about two weeks now since he officially retired. \n\nIt was right around then that I noticed this... sharper change. First my energy and my confidence just felt so different, and then that low, cloudy feeling kind of settled in. I really hope I don't sound like a terrible wife for saying that his being home all day might have triggered it! I love having him around, I really do, it's just... a big adjustment, I suppose. Is it normal for something like a retirement to cause a little slump like this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble with your memory?\n\nPatient: Memory? Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I certainly haven't forgotten any important meetings or misplaced my keys or anything dreadful like that! \n\nI suppose I might be just a tiny bit more forgetful than usual, but really, who isn't at my age? Sometimes I'll walk into a room and have to think for a second about what I went in there for, but it always comes right back to me. And like I said, my assistant has been helping me keep track of the little details at work, so nothing is slipping through the cracks. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything! I'm still perfectly capable of keeping everything organized. Does that sound alright? I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I've lost interest completely, no. I mean, I still love my hobbies and my family dearly! But... I suppose, um, I haven't really been wanting to go to my dance class the past couple of weeks. Or go look at the little galleries like I usually do. \n\nAnd... oh, I hate to even admit this, it makes me sound awful... but I haven't been cooking the big Sunday food for my family lately, either. I feel just terrible about it. Those are the things that usually make me feel like myself, you know? But I just haven't had the heart for them right this minute. \n\nI've just been telling everyone I'm taking a little break to adjust to my husband's new schedule. I really hope you don't think I'm being lazy or neglecting my family, Doctor! I promise I plan to get back to it all soon. Is it okay to just... step back from those things for a little bit while I'm feeling this way?\n\nDoctor: Since this cloudy feeling started, has your appetite gone down?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I've completely stopped eating or anything drastic like that! I certainly don't want you to worry about me starving myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose my eating has been just a little bit off lately. I find that just having my morning coffee and maybe a few little bites of something is enough to carry me through most of the day. And then at night... well, I feel a little silly admitting this, but I just sort of pick at some tortillas or maybe a few sweets. And I don't even really enjoy them the way I usually would, I'm just kind of nibbling without much thought. \n\nI make sure I'm still getting something in my stomach, though! I know how important it is to keep my strength up, especially at my age. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope you don't think I'm developing terrible habits, I just haven't had my usual appetite for proper meals.\n\nDoctor: Since this cloudy feeling started, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Sleep? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm having any real trouble. I mean, I'm certainly not tossing and turning all night or pacing the halls! \n\nI suppose I might be waking up just a tiny bit earlier than I used to, but isn't that just what happens when you get a little older? I'll open my eyes and it's still dark out, but I just lie there quietly so I don't disturb anyone. And really, it's quite peaceful. I still feel like I'm getting enough rest to manage my properties and get through the day, so it hasn't been a bother at all. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm struggling with insomnia or anything like that. I'm managing quite well, really. Does that sound normal to you, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling guilty or like you’re letting people down?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I feel like a burden or anything quite that strong. I know my family loves me, and I certainly don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party! \n\nBut... I suppose, um, I have been feeling a little bit bad about some things at work lately. It's just... well, I manage these properties, you see, and sometimes I get so excited about making things nice for the residents. I think I might have spoken a bit too big recently, promising some improvements before I really sat down and checked the budget properly. \n\nI just... I keep thinking I've let them down by getting their hopes up. Nobody has actually complained or said I ruined anything! They're all very sweet people. But I just have this nagging feeling that I made a mess of it, even if I haven't. I feel so silly even mentioning it, it's probably nothing, right? I just want to do a good job for everyone. I hope you don't think I'm terrible at my work, Doctor. I really do try my best!\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! I mean, I would never actually do anything to harm myself. I really don't want you to worry about me in that way, because I absolutely wouldn't do that. I have no plan to do anything like that whatsoever. \n\nBut, um... well, I want to make sure I'm being a good patient and answering you properly. I guess... there have been just a few mornings recently where I had this... this very silly, scary little thought. Just a fleeting thing, really! I'd be lying there and just sort of thought that maybe... maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up at all. \n\nIt sounds so awful to say out loud! Please don't think I'm giving up on everything. I really, truly don't want to hurt myself, I promise you that. It's just that sometimes the mornings have felt a little heavy lately, and my mind just wandered somewhere it shouldn't have. That doesn't make me a terrible person, does it? I hope I'm not alarming you, Doctor, I just wanted to be completely honest so I'm giving you all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I'm moving like a turtle or anything! I definitely don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. I try very hard to stay active and keep up with everything. \n\nBut, um, I suppose... when I'm just at home, by myself, sometimes my legs do feel just a tiny bit heavy. Like I'm moving a little slower than I usually do. But then, when I'm at work, especially in meetings with the residents or contractors, it's almost the opposite! I catch myself tapping my pen or shifting around in my chair. I get this... this keyed up feeling, like I just can't quite sit still. \n\nIt's probably just nervous energy, right? Because I want the meetings to go well. I hope that doesn't make me sound too jittery or inconsistent. I'm really trying to give you the clearest picture I can, Doctor. Does that make sense, the way I described it?\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't use that word at all. Hopeless sounds so... so final, doesn't it? Like giving up completely, and I am certainly not doing that! I have so much to be thankful for, and I really try to look on the bright side of things. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes things feel a little overwhelming with work and the budget and everything, like we talked about. But I always believe things will work out eventually. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself or thinking nothing will ever get better. That's not me at all. I'm doing my best to keep a positive attitude, Doctor. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Over these past two weeks, have you been feeling this low, cloudy way nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes, I suppose so. I really try not to let it show, though! I always try to put on a smile, because I certainly don't want to be a burden or bring anyone else down. But if I'm being perfectly honest with you so you have all the right information... yes, it's been pretty much every day for about two weeks. \n\nOh, I feel terrible even mentioning this next part. Please don't think I'm an ungrateful wife, because he is a wonderful man. But... well, this sharper change in my energy and my confidence, and then that low, cloudy feeling settling in... it really all started right when my husband retired. Suddenly he was just... home all day long. \n\nI mean, I'm happy for him, I truly am! It's just been a very big adjustment, having him around the house all the time while I'm still trying to juggle my property management work. I hope it's okay that I shared that with you. I just wanted to make sure I gave you the exact timeline you were asking for. Am I being helpful, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Does this reaction feel much stronger than you would normally expect from your husband retiring?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really don't want to make it sound like I'm overreacting or anything. I mean, retirement is a wonderful milestone, isn't it? We should be celebrating! \n\nI suppose... maybe it has hit me a tiny bit harder than I expected? But I don't want you to think I'm being dramatic or making a mountain out of a molehill. It's just... having my routine changed so suddenly, and having him right there in the living room while I'm trying to review leases... it's just a little much sometimes. \n\nBut I'm sure most wives would feel a little thrown off, right? It's perfectly normal to need an adjustment period. I definitely wouldn't say my reaction is completely out of proportion or anything like that. I'm just trying to take it one day at a time. Does that seem like a reasonable way to look at it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Has this been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I wouldn't use the word distress. That sounds so severe, doesn't it? I really don't want to complain, because I know people have much bigger problems. I'm still getting to work every day, and I'm keeping up with things as best I can. \n\nI suppose... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details—it has made things a tiny bit more difficult at the office. I've just had to slow down my decisions a little bit. And I've been asking my assistant to double-check some of the vendor details for me, just to be safe. I even put off a couple of board items, only because I just... I don't quite trust my judgment right now, with this cloudy feeling. \n\nBut I'm still doing my job, and I'm trying very hard not to let it affect anyone else. It's just a little bump in the road, right? I mean, it's good that I'm being careful and having my assistant check things, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm handling it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since this cloudy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... weight? Well, I haven't actually stepped on a scale, so I couldn't say for sure about any numbers. I certainly don't want to look like I'm wasting away or anything! \n\nBut, I suppose... well, my eating has been a little bit off lately. Nothing to worry about, though! It's just that, during the day, I'll have my coffee and maybe just a few bites of something, and that seems to carry me through perfectly fine. I just haven't had much of an appetite for a big meal. \n\nAnd then at night, I'll just sort of... pick at things. A few tortillas, or maybe some sweets, even though I'm not really enjoying them the way I usually would. It's just easier than cooking a whole dinner, especially with my husband home and our routine being so different now. But I'm still eating every day, so that's the important thing, right? I'm making sure I have something in my stomach. Oh, well... "down" is such a strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't want to say I've been completely down or depressed. I know some people can't even get out of bed, and I'm definitely not like that!
